# Rule re-check against the 0.22 sources (#634)

[中文](recheck-0.22.md) · [Guarantee register](../guarantees.en.md) · [Session protocol specification](../protocol-session.md) · [Session trace contract](../formal-trace.en.md) · Issue: [#634](https://github.com/arcships/rutis/issues/634)

Status: first version. Date: 2026-11-15. Baseline: `origin/main` `d8a9061a`.

Generated by `node tools/formal-trace.mjs --write`; `--check` compares this page with `rules/trace-contract.md`, `rules/protocol-registry-1.md`, `rules/protocol-registry-2.md`, `rules/protocol-session.md`, `rules/protocol-events.md` and the data in `tools/formal-trace-semantic.mjs`. 5 partitions have been re-extracted semantically: the 44 rows of the session trace contract (`TRC-*`), rewritten whole, and the 165, 218, 350 and 262 rows of the protocol lists `registry-1`, `registry-2`, `session`, `events`, where the rule wording is still the issue snapshot's and the source, anchor, evidence and status are re-extracted. The other protocol partitions (runtime, remote) were re-checked for source, anchor, evidence and status; their rule text has not been re-extracted, and their per-rule tables are in the Chinese page.

## 1. Method

The protocol rules pass four checks, and the trace rows pass a fifth. Every check that can be automated is in the generated pages; the judgement is in `tools/formal-trace-recheck.mjs` and `tools/formal-trace-semantic.mjs`.

1. **The source still exists**: the source document is in the current tree and 0.19 / 0.22 did not remove what it describes. Removed mechanisms (compat format, `events-v2`, the `sync-wait` capability, node `export` / `import`, `Registration`, the access point, `Revocations`, `PeerRows`, `peer:` rows, `runtimes.remote`, `Transport`, `ChannelInfo`) are `withdrawn`, and the gap names the rule that replaced them.
2. **A current anchor**: session rules map onto the `SESS-*` ids of `docs/protocol-session.md` §11; a registry row maps onto the guarantee-register row the issue split; an event rule maps onto the event protocol specification's own section or §15 decision, a `SESS-*` rule where the session specification carries it, or a register row; for the remaining partitions the gap says which document covers the rule or why none does.
3. **The evidence resolves**: the paths, test names and line ranges of the evidence column are checked against the current tree; a rule with only a model, or without an implementation check, is `partial`.
4. **The status only moves down**: the issue snapshot is the ceiling. A rule is never raised to `verified` because a specification calls it current; current describes the rule, not its implementation evidence.
5. **The rule text is re-extracted** (trace rows only): the wording is the current source's, not the old design's, and the row names the `SESS-*` ids that carry it — or says why the session specification does not cover it.

No status moved because of the re-extraction itself in any partition: no test was added, the two trace rows that gained evidence citations (`TRC-LIMIT-2`, `TRC-MALFORMED-2`) already described that evidence in their wording, and each registry row keeps the status the re-check gave it (the issue snapshot is the ceiling). `TRC-GREET-3` moved from `unverified` to `not guaranteed` after #1111 removed an unreachable check: no implementation offers a required-capabilities entry point. `TRC-NG-4` is withdrawn: protocol 4 no longer has the `objects` capability it described (#1123).

## 5. Per-row re-extraction: protocol list `registry-1`

The rule wording of these 165 rows is the issue snapshot's (Chinese, `rules/protocol-registry-1.md`); it was not rewritten. Each row was re-extracted on four points: the guarantee-register row that is its current source, that row as the anchor, the current evidence for the split claim, and the status with its reason.

## 6. Per-row re-extraction: protocol list `registry-2`

The rule wording of these 218 rows is the issue snapshot's (Chinese, `rules/protocol-registry-2.md`); it was not rewritten. Each row was re-extracted on four points: the guarantee-register row that is its current source, that row as the anchor, the current evidence for the split claim, and the status with its reason.

| Id | Status | Current source | Anchor | Evidence | Status and source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

## 7. Per-row re-extraction: protocol list `session`

These 350 rows are the issue's session section (SES, VAL, TAG, M1, CHN); their rule wording is the issue snapshot's and was not rewritten. Each row re-extracts four things: the current source (a `SESS-*` rule of the session specification §11, a guarantee-register row, a design section that is still current, or `0.22 has no such rule`), the current anchor (a `SESS-*` id or a register row), the current evidence for the rule, and the status with its reason. The status is the re-check's and only moves down.

| Id | Status | Current source | Anchor | Evidence | Status and source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SES-1-1 | partial | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-52, SESS-46 | `crates/rutis-bridge/src/session/testing.rs::session`（hold / fire / drop） | The session specification carries this in `SESS-52` (objects travel by reference) and `SESS-46` (reference wire format and identity); the issue marked it partial, and only function references have a cross-implementation test, object references (`Kind::Object`) have none. |
| SES-1-2 | partial | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-46, SESS-52 | `crates/rutis-bridge/tests/value_roundtrip.rs::rust_releases_balance`、`::node_releases_balance`、`::python_releases_balance`（返回值里的函数） | `SESS-46`/`SESS-52` allow references in arguments, returns and event payloads; the issue marked it partial: Future and object references in returns and events have no formal test. |
| SES-1-3 | withdrawn | 0.22 has no such rule | — (0.22 has no Rust runner: a Rust plugin runs in the host process itself, so there are no two runner kinds) | — | The runner design was never implemented (#121); the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-1-4 | withdrawn | design-multilang-m1-2026-10-04.md §十四 | — (The session specification does not cover it: M1 §十四 announces services that were provided without a declaration, so the “zero changes” exception is gone) | — | M1 §十四 and value design V0 mean a plugin need not be rewritten for the process boundary; the issue marked it replaced and 0.22 no longer states the limitation. |
| SES-1-5 | withdrawn | design-events-protocol-2026-10-18.md §十 | — (The session specification does not cover events: the event protocol specification §10 states the value and object rules) | — | The unified event protocol took over event values and objects, which SES §十一 itself says it superseded; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-2-1 | withdrawn | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-38 | — | 0.22 allows a synchronous wait: an invocation frame carries `sync` and the waiting side blocks while it pumps frames (`SESS-38`), so “always asynchronous” no longer holds; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-2-2 | unverified | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-52 | — | `SESS-52` sends objects with methods by reference rather than serialising them; the issue marked it uncovered, and the Node and Python object and dataclass behaviour has scattered ordinary tests only. |
| SES-2-3 | withdrawn | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-6 | — | `get` is a live read and may carry `sync` (`SESS-6` lists the frame fields); the immutable-snapshot / asynchronous-getter restriction is gone; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-2-4 | withdrawn | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-46 | — | There are no permission views: reference identity is the session plus the reference number with `home` for the original (`SESS-46`), so comparing across views has no subject; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-2-5 | verified | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-52 | `crates/rutis-bridge/src/session/testing.rs::session`（apply：回调在原进程执行） | `SESS-52` sends functions and objects by reference, never their source; the issue marked it covered, and the `apply` case of all seven implementations is the test above. |
| SES-2-6 | withdrawn | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-51 | — | The session layer clears the reference table when the session ends (`SESS-51`); unloading a row is a loader row rule; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-2-7 | partial | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-52, SESS-6 | `crates/rutis-bridge/src/session/testing.rs::malformed`（未知 op 结束会话） | The frames are `call` / `get` / `await` only (`SESS-6`), and a proxy exposes no field writes, prototype chain or `instanceof` (`SESS-52`); the issue marked it partial because only an ordinary test checks that `instanceof` does not hold. |
| SES-2-8 | withdrawn | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-6 | — | There are no interface descriptors or materialisation; `get` is a live read (`SESS-6`); the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-4-1 | withdrawn | design-multilang-m1-2026-10-04.md §五 | — (The session specification does not cover it: a managed plugin is a loader row plus a row inside the runtime (M1 §五), not a proxy fiber plus a runner fiber) | — | A managed plugin is now a loader row and a row inside the runtime (M1 §五); the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-4-2 | withdrawn | guarantees.md §14 | loader/announced-services, loader/rows-gate-on-services | — | A service slot is replaced or withdrawn per M1 §5.3, and a row that depends on it is stopped by the loader gating rules (`loader/announced-services`, `loader/rows-gate-on-services`); the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-4-3 | withdrawn | 0.22 has no such rule | — (There is no activation or execution gate: loading is `rows.load` / `rows.unload`, with no stage a host start has to open) | — | `rows.load` / `rows.unload` replaced activation; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-4-4 | withdrawn | 0.22 has no such rule | — (There is no activation: loading by generation and a late `rows.ended` replaced it (#625)) | — | A local refresh no longer has an “old activation”; generations and a late `rows.ended` are #625; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-4-5 | withdrawn | guarantees.md §14 | loader/rows-gate-on-services | — | A row whose dependency went away stops by the loader gating rule and reports `rows.ended`; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-5-1 | withdrawn | 0.22 has no such rule | — (There is no runner group: one runtime per language, with more started when isolation is needed (multilanguage runtimes design §四)) | — | There is one runtime per language and no shared runner; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-5-2 | withdrawn | guarantees.md §14 | loader/reload-all-or-nothing | — | There is no runner group: `loader/reload-all-or-nothing` states the reload semantics, without dropping the module cache to pretend an unload; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-5-3 | withdrawn | 0.22 has no such rule | — (There is no Rust runner or factory table, so a missing `factory` has no subject) | — | There is no Rust runner or factory table; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-5-4 | withdrawn | design-multilang-m1-2026-10-04.md §5.1 | — (The session specification does not cover it: `rows.schema` reads `inject` from the loaded module without running the plugin (M1 §5.1)) | — | `rows.schema` and M1 §5.1 replaced the prepare / build stages; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-5-5 | withdrawn | 0.22 has no such rule | — (There is no runner package directory: a row points at an npm package, a subpath or a `file://` path) | — | The path rules went with the runner; a row points at a package or a path directly; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-5-6 | withdrawn | guarantees.md §14 | loader/rows-gate-on-services | — | Static dependencies, waiting and cycle detection are M1 §5.2 and `loader/rows-gate-on-services`; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-6-1 | withdrawn | design-multilang-m1-2026-10-04.md §十四 | — (The session specification does not cover it: there is no interface descriptor, and the shape comes from `provides` in `rows.schema` or is inferred (M1 §十四)) | — | The interface descriptor was never implemented; M1 §十四 derives the shape from `provides` or infers it; the issue marked it replaced. |
| SES-6-2 | unverified | protocol-session.md §11 | SESS-52, SESS-74 | — | A cyclic object graph is broken by references (`SESS-52`), and values are limited to 64 levels (`SESS-74`); the issue marked it uncovered, and no language has a random check. |

## 8. Per-row re-extraction: protocol list `events`

These 262 rows are the issue's events section (EVP, EVD): 253 current and 9 replaced, with the rule wording left as the issue snapshot's. Each row re-extracts four things: the current source (a section or decision of the event protocol specification, a `SESS-*` rule of the session specification §11, a guarantee-register row, or `0.22 has no such rule`), the current anchor (`EVP` / `EVD` section or decision number, a `SESS-*` id, a register row), the current evidence (the conformance suite's event cases, the TLA+ invariants and mutation configurations under `specs/events`, a named Rust test), and the status with its reason. The status is the re-check's and only moves down. The event protocol specification is a 0.18 baseline: §2.1's `events-v2`, §2.2, the `events` field of §2.4 and §9's `export` / `import` were replaced by 0.19, and those rows are withdrawn.

## 9. What is still not re-extracted

- The 495 current protocol rules and the 62 replaced rows of `rules/protocol-*.md`, except `registry-1`, `registry-2`, `session` and `events`: the re-check maps them onto sources, anchors, evidence and status; it does not rewrite them. Their per-rule table is Chinese only.
- The rule wording of the 165 `registry-1`, 218 `registry-2`, 350 `session` and 262 `events` rows: source, anchor, evidence and status are re-extracted, the wording is the issue snapshot's, and the tables above do not translate it.
- The 219 guarantee-register rows: generated from `docs/guarantees.md`, so the register's wording is the source, not a re-extraction.
- The 10 `ECA-*` rows: hand-written proposals of the event-close admission design, with no current specification behind them yet.
- The English table of the remaining protocol partitions: a gap (#634).
